115年 9月全國賦稅收入初步統計 
一、全國賦稅收入 
（一）本月數 
9 月實徵淨額 4,965 億元，較上年同月增加 813 億元(+19.6%)。 
（二）累計數 
累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 3 兆 5,714 億元，較上年同期增加 7,004 億
元(+24.4%)。 
（三）達成率 
1.中央政府總預算業於 9 月 18 日公布，稅課收入較原預算案提高
6,000 億元，併計地方政府及特種基金後，全國稅課收入較預算案
提高 5,852 億元，本次發布均改依法定數計算預算達成率(實徵淨
額占全年預算數)。 
2.累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額占累計分配預算數 115.0%，占全年預算數
78.8%。

二、主要稅目別變動
（一）9 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以營業稅增加 297 億元、營利事業
所得稅增加 254 億元及證券交易稅增加 222 億元較多。
（二）累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 3,077
億元、營利事業所得稅增加 1,853 億元及營業稅增加 1,203 億元較多。
圖 2 主要稅目別變化
(115 年 1-9 月)
1.營利事業所得稅
(1)9 月實徵淨額 2,007 億元，較上年同月增加 254 億元(+14.5%)，主
因暫繳稅款成長。
(2)累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 1 兆 28 億元，較上年同期增加 1,853 億元
(+22.7%)，主因結算申報自繳 、暫繳稅款 俱增及延分期稅款陸續
入帳。
2.綜合所得稅
9 月實徵淨額 490 億元，較上年同月增加 34 億元(+7.4%)，主因扣繳
稅款增加所致；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 8,279 億元，較上年同期增加
790 億元(+10.6%)，主因結算申報自繳增加，以及盈餘分配、薪資所

得等扣繳稅款均呈成長。
3.營業稅
9月實徵淨額1,414億元，較上年同月增加297億元(+26.6%)，累計1至
9月實徵淨額6,230億元，較上年同期增加1,203億元(+23.9%)，均因內
需穩定，加以電子零組件 、資通與視聽產品 等進口代徵稅額增加 ，
惟出口暢旺，適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。

### Page 3

3
4.貨物稅
(1)9月實徵淨額100億元，較上年同月減少11億元(-10.0%)，主因汽柴
油減徵幅度擴大，惟電動車大量到港推升進口 車稅額，抵銷部分
減幅。
(2)累計1至9月實徵淨額874億元，較上年同期減少207億元(-19.2%)，
除汽柴油降稅因素外 ，亦與新購汽機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增
加有關。
5.證券交易稅
(1)9 月及累計 1 至 9 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 317 億
元、1 兆 1,461 億元，分別較上年同期(6,006 億元、4,451 億元)增
71.8%、1.6 倍。
(2)9 月 證 交 稅 實 徵 淨 額534 億 元， 較 上 年 同 月 增 加 222 億 元
(+71.2%)；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 5,060 億元，較上年同期增加
3,077 億元(+1.6 倍)。
6.關稅
9 月實徵淨額 157 億元，較上年同月增加 3 億元(+1.9%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 1,224 億元，較上年同期增加 80 億元(+7.0%)，均因機械

及電機設備、基本金屬及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
9 月實徵淨額 23 億元，較上年同月增加 6 億元(+35.7%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 211 億元，較上年同期增加 43 億元(+25.3%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
9 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 12 億元(+26.6%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 504 億元，較上年同期減少
14 億元(-2.8%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.印花稅
9 月實徵淨額 31 億元，較上年同月增加 4 億元(+16.2%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 175 億元，較上年同期增加 15 億元(+9.0%)，主因銀行放
款稅額成長。
（三）前 9月主要稅目實徵淨額占分配預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%者有證交稅(272%)、遺產稅(155%)、贈與稅(132%)、印花
稅(130%)、營業稅(116%)、關稅(114%)、契稅(111%)、房屋稅(106%)、

綜所稅(101%)、牌照稅(101%)及營所稅(101%)等。
2.低於 100%者有貨物稅(98%)、菸酒稅(97%)、土增稅(85%)、地價稅
(84%)及特銷稅(57%)等。

(+12.1%)，雖新版財政收支劃分法 開始施行，營業稅劃歸中央政府 比
率大幅調降，減少 2,748 億元(-90.7%)，惟證交稅、所得稅分別增加 3,077
億元、2,230 億元，互抵後呈現上升。

納入中央統籌分配 之營業稅

上年同期增加 2 億元(+0.8%)；房地合一課徵所得稅撥入住宅基金 135
億元，較上年同期減少 7 億元(-4.7%)；撥入長照基金 883 億元(其中 541

億元來自房地合一課徵所得稅、195 億元來自菸稅，146 億元來自遺贈
稅)，較上年同期增加 8 億元(+0.9%)。

## Document 023: 20260930_投資週報_經濟動能穩健，延續科技輪動行情

Source PDF: all report pdf/20260930_投資週報_經濟動能穩健，延續科技輪動行情.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資週報)
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同
意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 30 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國企業投資動能維持韌性 ：8 月標普全球美國綜合 PMI 升至 58.4，創2021 年 7 月來高點 ，
製造業、服務業同步擴張至 57 和 58.7； 8 月扣除飛機與國防的核心資本財訂單月增 1.6%，
高於前月修正後的 0.6%。其中，初級金屬、機械及電機設備訂單均呈現成長，顯示企業資本
支出、AI 投資與國防需求仍具韌性，持續支撐美國經濟成長。
◼ 美中同意貿易休兵再延長兩個月：美中同意調降雙邊總值 600 億美元商品的關稅，涵蓋美國

玉米、化妝品及中國家電、玩具等產品，並延長貿易休兵至明年 1 月 10 日，同時中國承諾繼
續供應稀土並擴大採購美國農產品與煤礦，美國則繼續暫停實施 50%穿透性規則；AI 方面，
美中雙方雖未就 AI 監管議題達成共識 ， 但同意建立風險溝通機制 ， 並於11 月底前展開對話 。
◼ Meta 旗下 AI 代理人 Muse 登頂排行榜：Meta 新款個人 AI 代理 Muse 上線後迅速累積逾
250 萬次下載，並登上美國 App Store 下載排行榜首位。相較過去生成式 AI 以問答與內容生
成為主，Muse 可進一步協助安排日程、搜尋資訊、處理文件及完成交易，代表 AI 正從提升
效率工具逐步走向數位勞動力，市場評價焦點也將逐漸由 AI 基礎設施擴散至應用軟體業者。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，科技供應鏈成長動能延續：AI 基礎建設需求持續擴張，帶動 HBM、伺服器
DRAM、 先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持成長動能 ， 市場表現由全面上漲逐步轉向結構性分
化。若 AI 伺服器需求持續成長且 CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優勢、定價能力及較高訂單

能見度之企業，其營運表現有機會獲得支撐。整體而言，科技產業獲利表現仍具關注度，惟後
續仍須留意全球景氣、產業供需變化、政策監管及市場評價調整等風險因素。
◼ 債券佈局可關注中天期美國公債及投等債：中天期美國公債及投資級債仍值得持續關注：**雖
然貨幣政策風險下降有助於穩定長期通膨預期 ， 但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應 ，
以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，
中天期債券於票息收益與存續期間風險之間相對較為均衡。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公
司債指數利差約 80 個基點，雖然利差偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供相對較高的收益水準。
因此，在目前市場環境下，中天期美國公債及投資級債仍為值得持續觀察的債券資產類別，惟
後續表現仍須留意利率走勢、信用利差變化及經濟成長動能等因素。

年9 月9 日

如何理解价格数据的回落？，2026 年 8 月
9 日

PPI 环比转负，下半年物价表现或回归基
本面，2026 年7 月9 日

如何看待PPI-CPI 剪刀差走扩？ 
        ——2026 年9 月价格数据点评

事件：2026 年 9 月全国居民消费价格指数（CPI）同比 0.9%（前
值 0.8%），环比 0.2%（前值 0.4%），1-9 月 CPI 同比 0.9%（前值
0.9%）；核心 CPI 同比 1%（前值 1%），环比0（前值 0.1%），1-9 月
核心 CPI 同比 1.1%（前值 1.1%）；工业生产者出厂价格指数（PPI）
同比5%（前值3.8%），环比1.2%（前值0.4%），1-9 月PPI 同比2.3%
（前值2%）。 
受油价、芯片等涨价影响，CPI 环比连续两个月为正,9 月略高于
季节性水平，CPI 同比涨幅较上月小幅扩大；1-9 月，CPI 同比及核心
CPI 同比均较上年底 回升但仍低于上半年水平 ，价格持续修复 但修复
节奏放缓。9 月 CPI 同比增长 0.9%，较上月回升 0.1 个百分点，连续
两个月回升，环比延续正增长， 为 0.2%，强于季节性水平（2023 年-
2025 年，9 月 CPI 环比均值为 0.1%）。9 月核心 CPI（剔除食品和能
源）同比增长 1%，与上月持平，仍位于较低水平， 环比 0，持平季节
性水平（2023 年-2025 年，9 月核心CPI 环比均值为0）。1-9 月，CPI
同比及核心 CPI 同比分别为 0.9%和 1.1%，均与 1-8 月持平，较上年底
和上年同期水平回升，但均较上半年回落 0.1 个百分点，仍位于较低
区间水平，价格修复的基础有待进一步夯实。 
 油价仍是 CPI 环比维持正增的重要支撑， 同时受成本走高影响， 本
月移动电话机、计算机、数据存储设备和平板电脑 价格环比上涨、
对CPI 环比维持正增带来一定助力。工业消费品中，受国际市场价格
变动影响，9 月国内汽油价格环比上涨4.9%，连续两个月环比正增，
油价仍是CPI 环比维持正增的重要支撑。受芯片等成本上涨影响，从
7 月开始，多家手机厂商已经陆续上调部分在售产品价格。9 月 1 日，

2026 年9 月价格数据点评  
如何看待PPI-CPI 剪刀差走扩？ 
华为、小米、荣耀多款手机官方调整售价，单次涨幅从 200 元至 1000 元不等。9 月移动电话
机、计算机、数据存储设备和平板电脑价格环比分别上涨 3.7%、2.0%、1.7%和 1.5%，合计影
响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04 个百分点。此外，秋装上市，带动服装价格环比上涨 0.7%，影响 CPI
环比上涨约0.03 个百分点。 
 暑期效应消退，服务价格环比由涨转降，持平季节性水平，对 CPI 环比的影响由上月支撑转
为拖累，其中飞机票、宾馆住宿等 假期出行相关服务价格环比均由正转负。 9 月，服务价格
同比上涨0.8%，涨幅与上月相同，服务价格环比由上月上涨0.1%转为下降0.2%，持平季节性
（2023-2025 年9 月，服务价格环比均值为-0.2%），对CPI 环比的影响由上月上拉约0.04 个
百分点转为下拉约 0.08 个百分点。具体来看，随着暑假结束，飞机票、宾馆住宿环比均由正
转负，飞机票、宾馆住宿和旅行社收费价格环比分别下降 8.7%、7.0%和 6.0%，合计影响 CPI
环比下降约0.12 个百分点。 
 食品价格环比维持正增长 ，但低于季节性水平 ，同比降幅边际扩大，食品价格仍偏弱 。9 月
食品价格同比下降 1.7%，降幅比上月扩大 0.3 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.29 个百分
点，环比上涨0.4%，连续两个月正增长，影响CPI 环比上涨约0.06 个百分点，但仍低于季节
性水平（2023-2025 年 9 月，食品价格环比均值为 0.6%）。其中，受猪肉供给结构调整以及
暑假、中秋和国庆备货需求带动，鲜菜及猪肉价格环比连续 3 个月为正。此外，休渔期结束，
水产品价格环比连续两个月为负。

如何看待PPI-CPI 剪刀差走扩？ 
受输入性因素、 上游原材料行业供需关系调整 、人工智能行业快速发展提振相关行业需
求等带动，9 月PPI 环比连续两个月正增长，同比5%、升至2022 年7 月以来高点。1-9 月PPI
同比2.3%，较上半年上行0.8 个百分点，为2023 年以来的高点。9 月PPI 同比5%，较上月上
行 1.2 个百分点，PPI 环比连续两个月维持正增，9 月环比上涨 1.2%。PPI 分项中，生产资料
价格同比涨幅较上月扩大 1.5 个百分点，为 6.5%，是 PPI 同比上行的重要支撑，环比连续两
个月正增，为1.4%；生活资料价格同比降幅连续3 个月收窄，9 月较上月收窄0.4 个百分点，
为-0.1%，环比连续两个月正增，9 月为0.1%。 
 受中东局势与全球 AI 快速发展影响，国际油价、有色等大宗商品价格上涨带动国内相关行业
价格上涨，是 9 月 PPI 环比上行最重要的支撑；同时行业供需结构调整叠加季节性因素带动
煤炭、非金属矿物制品等部分行业价格上涨；人工智能快速发展，AI 产业链部分需求增加、
价格上涨，上述因素均对 PPI 形成支撑。从行业来看，受原油、有色金属等海外定价大宗商

如何看待PPI-CPI 剪刀差走扩？ 
品价格上行影响，石油开采、精炼石油产品制造、有机化学原料制造价格环比分别上涨14.5%、
7.4%和5.4%，有色金属矿采选业、有色金属冶炼和压延加工业价格环比分别上涨1.6%和1.5%，
5 个行业合计影响PPI 环比上涨约0.63 个百分点，是本月PPI 环比改善最重要的支撑。同时，
受冬季供暖备煤需求增加、主产矿区供应阶段性偏紧 等因素影响，煤炭开采和洗选业、煤炭
加工价格环比分别上涨9.5%和13.6%。9 月是建筑施工旺季，钢材、水泥等建筑材料需求有所
回升，加之 煤炭等原材料成本上涨，非金属矿物制品业 （主要包括水泥、玻璃等） 价格环比
由负转正，上涨0.3%，黑色金属冶炼和压延加工业价格环比上涨0.2%，4 个行业合计影响PPI
环比上涨约 0.38 个百分点。此外，人工智能、算电协同等领域加快发展，电气机械和器材制
造业、计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业等 AI 产业链相关行业价格环比均上涨 0.4%，亦对
PPI 上行带来支撑。  
 生活资料价格环比连续两个月为正但仍位于低位区间，同比降幅收窄至 0 附近。生活资料价
格同比降幅连续 3 个月收窄但仍为负，9 月同比下降 0.1%，环比延续 8 月的正增长态势、连
续两个月为正，不过仍位于0 左右的低位区间，终端需求仍偏弱。

如何看待PPI-CPI 剪刀差走扩？

整体来看，今年以来物价整体呈现温和修复态势，呈现以下特征：一是上下游景气度存
在温差，中下游尤其下游中小企业仍在承受成本挤压 。今年以来，生产资料与生活资料同比
分化走势逐渐加大，二者剪刀差由年初 1 月的 0.4 个百分点扩大到 9 月的 6.6 个百分点。结
合 PMI 数据来看，截至 9 月，主要原材料购进价格分项与出厂价格剪刀差连续两个月扩大。
涨价能力和利润持续向上游资源行业集中，终端消费品价格整体仍偏弱，中下游制造业面临
成本挤压。二是 AI 产业链涨价正逐步成为影响物价走势的新增变量。从电气机械、算力设
施等上游环节，到存储器件、平板终端、手机等下游消费品，AI 相关产品价格表现均较好，
成为价格修复的重要力量。三是当前经济仍面临供给端涨价与需求端疲软并存的矛盾，PPI-
CPI 剪刀差扩大。自今年 4 月 PPI 同比首次超过 CPI 同比以来，PPI-CPI 正剪刀差由 4 月的
1.6%波动扩大至 9 月的 4.1%，9 月较 8 月扩大 1.1 个百分点。供需失衡的格局仍在延续，价
格修复仍受到掣肘。 
后续来看，9 月以来中东局势再度升级，国际原油价格波动上行，截至10 月13 日，WTI
原油期货结算价由 9 月底的 90.42 美元/桶，波动上行至 94.1 美元/桶。若油价持续高位运
行，短期石化产业链价格仍将位于较高水平， PPI 同比保持韧性仍有支撑，并沿产业链向
CPI 能源分项传导。但也要看到，当前生产端价格改善仍主要集中于能源、有色和部分新动
能行业，终端需求相关行业价格仍偏弱，核心CPI 始终在1%左右的较低水平徘徊，全面物价
上涨的可能性较低，完成2%的年度通胀目标难度仍较大。

数据来源：中诚信国际整理 数据来源：中诚信国际整理

營 運 分 析

旺宏為全球 NOR Flash領導廠商 
旺宏是全球領先的 NOR Flash 製造商，提供涵蓋廣泛規格和容量的ROM唯
讀記憶體、NOR型及 NAND型快閃記憶體解決方案。目前公司的產能包括8
吋晶圓產能為每月 45-48 千片，12 吋晶圓產能為每月 20 千片，而 ROM 主
要透過外購再進行加工。公司還提供ROM、NOR型快閃記憶體及 NAND型
快閃記憶體的解決方案，並從事次微米邏輯製程、高壓 CMOS 及 BCD製程
的晶圓代工服務，包括嵌入式非揮發性記憶體的 BCD 及邏輯製程。公司的
廠房位於新竹科學園區，包含生產8吋晶圓的晶圓二廠、12吋晶圓的晶圓五
廠，以及產品測試廠。26Q3產品組合：NAND 47%、NOR 46%、ROM 5%、
FBG 2%。

26Q4 eMMC、NOR FLASH需求維持強勁 
展望 26Q4 預期 NAND、eMMC、NOR FLASH 等產品合約價將持續漲價，
然漲價幅度將較 26H1 放緩且目前稼動率已高出貨量於新增產能擴出前成長
有限。因此兆豐預估營收 280.66 億元，QoQ+15.8%，YoY+263.1%，毛利
率 74.6%，EPS 6.93元。

圖三、26Q4產品漲價有利毛利提升 
旺宏各季營收及獲利走勢預估(單位：百萬)

資料來源：旺宏；兆豐投顧預估

NOR Flash 需求穩步上升，車用與伺服器市場成為成長主力 
整體 NOR Flash 產品將持續成長，特別是在車用和伺服器應用方面。隨著汽
車電子化和自動駕駛技術的發展，汽車行業對高可靠性、耐高溫的半導體需
求不斷增加，有利 NOR Flash 在車用市場用量持續增加。此外，伺服器市場
的需求也隨著數據中心的擴展而逐步上升。NOR Flash的快速響應和高可靠
性特性使其非常適合用於伺服器中的關鍵啟動代碼和固件應用。預期旺宏車
規 eMMC 預期於 2027 年開始小量出貨，2028 年放量，增添公司營運動能。

eMMC需求相對強勁，成獲利回溫關鍵 
展望 2026-2027 年，受惠原廠減產趨勢導致 eMMC 需求強勁，車規 eMMC
預期於 2027 年開始小量出貨，2028 年放量，增添公司營運動能，且 NOR 
FLASH 受惠 AI 帶動，需求強勁然後續價格漲幅有限，因應 NAND 及 NOR 
FLASH客戶強勁需求，預期 27H2 年新產能開出，將再增強公司擴力表現。
因此兆豐預估 2026/2027 營收 818.97/1229.03 億元，YoY+183.6/50.1%，
毛利率 66.7/74.1%，稅後淨利 342.1/582.8 億元，EPS 為 17.25/29.39 元。

記憶體持續供不應求，然漲價力道放緩，調降為「逢低買進」投資評等 
旺宏目前位於 3.5X 27(F)PER)，基於 eMMC 及 NOR強勁需求，然漲價力道
有限，調降為「逢低買進」投資評等，目標價 120 元(4.1 x 2 7(F)PER)，潛
在上漲空間 16.5%。

長線來看，車用與伺
服器應用成為 NOR 
Flash成長動能 
漲價力道有限，調降
為「逢低買進」投資
評等,目標價 120元。 
長期看好 NOR 及
eMMC 產品線持續成
長

## Document 114: 261020_ml_nienmade

Source PDF: all report pdf/261020_ml_nienmade.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Nien Made Enterprise

3Q preview: EPS +13% YoY to NT$6.6; 
Better 2H but not yet a turnaround

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 441.00 TWD | Price: 358.50 TWD

3Qe EPS NT$6.6: strong rev and GP offset by opex and FX 
Nien Made is scheduled to report 3Q26 results on 4 Nov. Disclosed monthly sales imply 
3Q26 revenue growth of 16% YoY to NT$8.5bn, or 9% in USD terms, largely 
volume-driven. Growth reaccelerated from 1.7% YoY in 1H26, led by custom-made on a 
low US base and expansion in Europe and Japan. Ready-made also improved as 
Cambodia labor shortages eased. (See Exhibit 1 for US window coverings spending 
trends). Gross margin: We expect underlying GPM to rise 1.1ppt YoY to 59.8% on lower 
tariffs, broadly flat QoQ. We forecasts an NT$300mn of IEEPA tariff refunds in 2H26. 
Assuming NT$150mn is booked in 3Q, this would add 1.7ppt and lift reported GPM to 
61.5%. We expect the selling expense ratio to fall 1.7ppt QoQ to 17.5% on lower fuel 
charges, but remain 0.5ppt higher YoY due to continued salesforce and distribution 
network investment. We expect an FX loss in 3Q26 vs. an NT$118mn gain in 3Q25, 
weighing on YoY earnings growth. We forecast 3Q26 EPS of NT$6.6, up 13% YoY. 
US window covering picking up, but no turnaround yet  
BofA credit/debit card data suggests US spending on window coverings turned positive 
in Aug-26 for the first time after 19 consecutive months of YoY declines. Spending rose 
4%/3% YoY in Aug/Sep, respectively. This improvement partly explains the recent 
reacceleration in Nien Made's monthly sales. That said, we view the improvement more 
as normalization off a low base than the start of an industry turnaround. The Aug-Sep 
2025 comparison base was relatively weak, while the current rate-hike environment 
remains a headwind to housing-related demand. 
Slightly raise FY26 EPS est. to NT$22.9; PO to NT$441 
We raise our FY26 EPS estimate by 2% to NT$22.9 to reflect the blended impact of 
stronger-than-expected revenue growth and FX loss. We maintain our FY26 DPS forecast 
of NT$17 per share. We raise our PO by 4% to NT$441, as we roll forward our valuation 
basis from FY26E EPS to FY27E EPS. At the same time, we lower our target P/E from 
19x to 17x, reflecting the MSCI removal and a less supportive rate environment.

* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 7.

Company Sector 
Interior Furnishings

Company Description 
Nien Made, founded in 1974, is one of the world's largest 
window-covering manufacturers. It has roughly 50% 
market share in both ready-made (OEM business) shades 
and blinds, and custom-made (own brand) shutters in the 
US. The US market accounted for over 75% of sales. Key 
clients include Home Depot and Wal-Mart for ready-made 
shades and blinds. Nien Made operates its own retail 
network and e-commerce business for its custom-made 
shutters under the Norman brand name in the US and 
Europe.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on Nien Made, mainly given 1) clear 
custom-made growth profile (share gain in the US and fast-
growing DTC in Japan and Europe), 2) its largest customer 
Home Depot is gaining market share, and 3) margin 
expansion potentials backed by favorable products mix.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Nien Made Enterprise (XNRBF) 
Our PO NT$441 is derived from 17x P/E 2027E EPS. This is around 0.5SD above 
historical the one-year forward P/E, which we think is warranted given the visibility of 
the growth profile.

Downside risks are 1) weaker-than-expected Home Depot's sell-through, 2) slow 
recovery of US housing turnover, and 3) higher tariff increases on China exports to the 
US. 4) Loss of key customers.

 Measures Definitions 
Business Performance Numerator Denominator 
Return On Capital Employed NOPAT = (EBIT + Interest Income) × (1 − Tax Rate) + Goodwill Amortization Total Assets − Current Liabilities + ST Debt + Accumulated Goodwill 
Amortization 
Return On Equity Net Income Shareholders’ Equity 
Operating Margin Operating Profit Sales 
Earnings Growth Expected 5 Year CAGR From Latest Actual N/A 
Free Cash Flow Cash Flow From Operations − Total Capex N/A  
Quality of Earnings Numerator Denominator 
Cash Realization Ratio Cash Flow From Operations Net Income 
Asset Replacement Ratio Capex Depreciation 
Tax Rate Tax Charge Pre-Tax Income 
Net Debt-To-Equity Ratio Net Debt = Total Debt − Cash & Equivalents Total Equity 
Interest Cover EBIT Interest Expense  
Valuation Toolkit Numerator Denominator 
Price / Earnings Ratio Current Share Price Diluted Earnings Per Share (Basis As Specified) 
Price / Book Value Current Share Price Shareholders’ Equity / Current Basic Shares 
Dividend Yield Annualised Declared Cash Dividend Current Share Price 
Free Cash Flow Yield Cash Flow From Operations − Total Capex Market Cap = Current Share Price × Current Basic Shares 
Enterprise Value / Sales EV = Current Share Price × Current Shares + Minority Equity + Net Debt + 
Other LT Liabilities 
Sales 
EV / EBITDA Enterprise Value Basic EBIT + Depreciation + Amortization 
iQmethod SMis the set of BofA Global Research standard measures that serve to maintain global consistency under three broad headings: Business Performance, Quality of Earnings, and validations. The key features of 
iQmethod are: A consistently structured, detailed, and transparent methodology. Guidelines to maximize the effectiveness of the comparative valuation process, and to identify some common pitfalls. 
iQdatabase ®  is our real-time global research database that is sourced directly from our equity analysts’ earnings models and includes forecasted as well as historical data for income statements, balance sheets, and cash 
flow statements for companies covered by BofA Global Research. 
iQprofile SM, iQmethod SM  are service marks of Bank of America Corporation. iQdatabase ® is a registered service mark of Bank of America Corporation.

Update
Accton Technology Corporation  | Asia Pacific
Announced Rights Issuance
What's new:
• Accton's BoD has approved  a rights issuance of up to  10mn common shares.
• Proceeds will be used for working capital and bank loan repayment.
Our view:
• The share dilution will be manageable at  up to ~2%.
• We view the recent correction (-10% vs. flat for Taiex over the past 2 trading
sessions) a good opportunity to accumulate positions, as the current

valuation of 15-16x 2027e P/E has approached the trough level since 2020
and the revenue and gross margin should continue to grow on a QoQ basis
into 4Q26.
• Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Accton Technology Corporation (2345.TW)
Base case, P/E, which we believe better captures the company's earnings growth momentum.
We assign a target P/E of 30x to our 2027 EPS estimate, which we view as justified by an
operating profit CAGR of 48% for 2025-28, based on our estimates. This implies a PEG of
around 0.6, vs. its average of 0.7 over 2019-25, when the stock was trading at a P/E of 22x for
an operating profit CAGR of 32%.

Risks to Upside
n Stronger data center capex
n Faster network switch spec migration
n InfiniBand losing share in AI networks
n Stronger custom AI accelerator growth
n Stronger ODM direct business model adoption
Risks to Downside
n Weaker data center capex
n Slower network switch spec migration
n InfiniBand taking more share in AI networks
n Softer custom AI accelerator growth
n Softer ODM direct business model adoption

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 133: 261027_daiwa_TSMT

Source PDF: all report pdf/261027_daiwa_TSMT.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

Background: We met with Taiwan Surface Mounting Technology (TSMT) 
on 27 October. According to management, its product mix for 3Q26 was 
TFT-LCD 47%, Mini LED 9%, DRAM module 15%, auto 10%, cable 7%, 
touch 4% and others 8%. TSMT is positive on its optical transceiver, DRAM 
module, auto, low-earth orbit satellites (LEO), server and robotic segments. 
The QFII holding in TSMT was 29.7% on 22 October vs. 19.8% at the end 
of 2025.

Highlights: 4Q26 and 2027 outlook update – an upward revision. 
TSMT guided for its 4Q26 revenue to grow by 5-8% QoQ, and 2027 
revenue to grow by at least 20% (some upside potential subject to raw 
material conditions) for 2027 thanks to multiple revenue drivers, including 
optical transceivers, DRAM module, auto, LEO, server and robotic 
segments. For 4Q26 and 2027, the company expects its quarterly revenue 
and gross margin to further expand sequentially on a more favourable 
product mix from optical transceiver, LEO and server revenue. TSMT 
expects its gross margin to improve to 15-17% in 1H27 and over 20% in 
2H27 on the solid optical transceiver order outlook.

Strong revenue and margin outlook is expected to be driven mainly 
by the optical transceiver segment. TSMT mentioned several revenue 
drivers for 2027-28; among them, optical transceiver surface mounting 
technology (SMT) assembly is the most significant one. TSMT is a major 
global supplier for 800G and 1.6T products. A US-based client has noted 
that the 1.6T delivery schedule was ahead of its previous expectation (from 
4Q26 to 3Q26). The company has thus won an additional US-based tier-
one client (expected to have a larger contribution than the first optical client 
from 3Q27) and a China-based client over the past few months. TSMT is 
also in talks with a new US-based tier-one optical transceiver client. 
According to TSMT, optical transceiver revenue will likely account for 5-
10% of total revenue in 1Q27, 10-15% in 2Q27 and 15-20% in 3Q27; for 
2028, the company aims for 30-40%. Given the pure outsourcing (ie, 
includes no buying and selling of material) nature of the optical transceiver 
business, the segment brings upside potential to the gross margin level, 
per management. Aside from the optical transceiver segment, the company 
also sees strong revenue momentum in 2027-2028 from the DRAM module 
(2027 revenue up 30+% YoY), auto, robotic, LEO and server segments. 
TSMT says it has been expanding its global capacity and adjusting its 
equipment to fit different usages, eg, transforming from TFT-LCD to optical 
transceiver.

Valuation: The stock is currently trading at PERs of 24.8x/13.8x, based on 
Bloomberg’s 2026E/27E EPS forecasts.

27 October 2026

## Document 134: 261027_daiwa_unimicron

Source PDF: all report pdf/261027_daiwa_unimicron.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: Our first take on Unimicron’s 3Q26 results (released on 27 October) is that 
earnings were better than expected, driven by stronger-than-expected non-operating 
gains. We attribute the upside primarily to a smaller-than-feared valuation loss on UMC in 
3Q26. In addition, the company mentioned that other investments, including Unimicron 
shares held through investment vehicles, contributed c.TWD3bn of mark-to-market gains in 
3Q26. As for the core business, 3Q26 revenue reached TWD52.7bn (up 23% QoQ, up 
55% YoY), with gross margin expanding 4.0ppt QoQ to 28.8% and operating margin rising 
4.5ppt QoQ to 20.0%, supported by improving profitability in ABF substrates and HDI. We 
believe the profitability performance was broadly in line with the latest buy-side 
expectations. Looking ahead, while the company does not provide numerical guidance, 
revenue and gross margin should continue to improve in 4Q26. Given Unimicron’s leading 
position across most CSP AI ASIC projects, Nvidia GPUs, and EMIB-T, we believe the 
company should continue to benefit from a favourable pricing environment amid the 
current supply-demand dynamics and ongoing specification upgrades, including larger 
substrate body sizes and technology migration. We have a Buy (1) rating on Unimicron 
with a 12M TP of TWD1,500, based on a target PER of 35x applied to our 1-year-forward 
EPS forecast. Key downside risk: weaker-than-expected demand.

Source: Company data, Daiwa forecasts, Bloomberg

What’s the impact: 
Below is our first take on Unimicron’s 3Q26 results (released on 27 October). We will 
review our model in due course.

• 3Q26 results beat expectations. Unimicron’s 3Q26 earnings came in at TWD10.1bn 
(EPS: TWD6.15), beating our estimate of TWD4.2bn and Bloomberg consensus of 
TWD6.7bn by 140% and 49%, respectively, mainly due to higher-than-expected non-
operating gains. In our previous note, Unimicron recognised c.TWD8bn of mark-to-
market gains on UMC (2303 TT, TWD122.5, Neutral [3], covered by Rick Hsu) in 2Q26. 
We had originally expected Unimicron to recognise c.TWD5bn of valuation losses in 
3Q26, given UMC’s share-price performance in July. However, the actual valuation loss 
of c.TWD0.7bn was substantially better than feared. In addition, the company attributed 
part of the strength to c.TWD3bn of other mark-to-market gains, including gains related 
to Unimicron shares held through its investment vehicles. This is broadly consistent with 
the reported 3Q26 total non-operating contribution of c.TWD2.6bn after accounting for 
the UMC valuation loss and other non-operating items.

 3Q26 results first-take: results beat on non-ops

### Page 2

Unimicron Technology Corp (3037 TT) 
27 October 2026 
2

• Looking at the core business, 3Q26 revenue reached TWD52.7bn (up 23% QoQ, up 
55% YoY), with gross margin expanding 4.0ppt QoQ to 28.8% and operating margin 
rising 4.5ppt QoQ to 20.0%, supported by improving profitability in ABF substrates and 
HDI. With stronger operating leverage, operating profit surged 58% QoQ to 
TWD10.53bn. We believe the gross-margin and operating-margin performance was 
broadly in line with recent buy-side expectations for modestly improving profitability in 
ABF substrates and HDI, given that ABF margin was already high at c.40% in 2Q26, 
alongside a higher revenue contribution from a large end customer. We estimate ABF 
substrate margin at c.42-44% in 3Q26 (vs. c.40% in 2Q26).

• Business outlook. While the company does not provide numerical guidance, revenue 
and gross margin should continue to improve in 4Q26 amid the current substrate supply 
shortage. In addition, the company is aggressively expanding capacity at its Guangfu 
and Yangmei plants over 2027-29, with roughly c.30% annual ABF capacity expansion, 
which should become a key growth driver for Unimicron. Given Unimicron’s leading 
position across most CSP AI ASIC projects, Nvidia GPUs, and EMIB-T, we believe the 
company should continue to benefit from a favourable pricing environment amid the 
current supply-demand dynamics and ongoing specification upgrades, including larger 
substrate body sizes and technology migration.

What we recommend: 
We have a Buy (1) rating on Unimicron with a 12M TP of TWD1,500, based on a target 
PER of 35x applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key downside risk: less-than-
expected demand.

未來佔比會下降，目前外包月產能一個月 5-6 台，因為外包產能受
限，同業也在搶外包產能。2027 主要成長動能仍為 OSAT，今年晶
圓代工客戶營收高於 OSAT，2027 年 OSAT 營收超過晶圓代工客戶，
SOIC 機台去年小量，今年量增，2027 跟 2026 差不多略增，2028
才會明顯大增，主要還是跟隨晶圓代工客戶 SOIC 擴廠進度。 
 2028-2029 年 PLP 機台出貨量會很明顯放大:PLP 及 CoPoS 設備機
台單價很高，初估有機會是 CoWoS 翻倍，PLP 進度比較快，PLP
規格弘塑 310*310、510*515、600*600 都有，再大到 700 以上就不
做了，晶圓代工客戶規格是 310*310 但目前進度比較慢，還在實驗
線階段。力成 510*515 進度最快，4Q26 會拉第一批機台，2027 隨
力成購置的友達廠進度持續拉機台，2028-2029 年 PLP 機台出貨量
會很明顯放大。 
 維持買進評等，目標價 3000 元：基本面無變化，預估數字也沒做調
整，故維持買進評等 ，預估 2026 年 EPS 64.81 元，2027 年 EPS 93.97
元，目標價 3000 元。(2027 PER 30x)

弘塑為本土封測設備龍頭廠商 
 弘塑為本土封測設備龍頭廠商 
弘塑為本土封測設備龍頭廠商，主要利基產品為半導體後段濕製程設備，並延
伸相關化學品、檢測設備代理及軟體解決方案。1H26 產品結構可區分為機台檢
測產品(65%)、化學產品(20%)、代理設備(15%)。2013 年合併化學品公司添鴻
(CLC)、2018 年底合併通路商佳霖(ClC)、併購資訊系統廠太引(Tyne)，投資美
國 X-Ray 檢測公司 SVXR。 客戶為國內半導體 、 光電 、記憶體及面板產業大廠，
包括台積電、矽品、日月光、力成、星科金朋、精材、聯電、茂矽、采鈺、穏
懋、中芯、宏達等。

2Q26 毛 利率 39.37%， 低於預
期，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元 
 2Q26 毛利率 39.37%，低於預期，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元 
弘塑 2Q26 EPS 11.18 元，低於元富預估的 12.34 元，獲利達成率 91%。本業
來看，2Q26 營收 17.27 億元，+8%QOQ，+6%YOY，2Q26 毛利率 39.37%，
較 1Q26 回升 5.1 個百分點，然仍低於預估的 41%，主要是外包成本及急單增
加影響部分毛利率，預期 3Q26 毛利率持續向上改善，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元。

弘塑 3Q26 營收 22.67 億元，+31.3%QOQ，+51.8%YOY，營收達成率 98%，
略低於預期，預估 3Q26 毛利率 41%，預估 3Q26 EPS 16.47 元。

營運逐季成長，OSA T展望樂觀

營運逐季成長，OSA T展望樂觀 
2026 年弘塑擴產一倍 ，1Q26 已經完成擴建 ，3Q26 新產能已經滿載。 外包部分 ，
目前外包比例 33%，未來佔比會下降，目前外包月產能一個月 5-6 台，因為外
包產能受限 ， 同業也在搶外包產能 。2027 年擴產再 50-100%， 以租用廠房為主。
2027 主要成長動能還是 OSAT，今年晶圓代工客戶營收高於 OSAT，2027 年
OSAT 營收超過晶圓代工客戶，SOIC 機台去年小量，今年量增，2027 跟 2026
差不多略增 ，2028 才會明顯大增 ， 主要還是跟隨晶圓代工客戶 SOIC 擴廠進度，
SOIC 機台種類 6-7 種。

## Document 260: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 
2026-10-14

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 820,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 366,500 원 
상승여력: 123.7%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 및 환율 영향으로 시장 예상치 하회 예상 
Key: 4Q26부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 계약 수주 모멘텀 기대 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 4 행정 엔진&FDC으로 주가 상승여력 
충분. 매수

3Q26 Preview: 컨센서스 하회하는 실적 기록 예상 
3Q26 연결 매출액 6조 464억원(+36.9% YoY, -4.5% QoQ), 영업이익 9,230억
원(+65.6% YoY, -11.2% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(1조 70억원)를 하회하는 실적 기
록할 것으로 추정한다. 이전 분기 대비 중대재해 및 하계휴가, 그리고 추석 연휴에 따른 
조업일수 감소 효과와 더불어 큰 폭의 환율 하락 영향(약 500억원) 때문이다. 홍범도
함 화재 및 임단협 관련 일회성 비용은 이번 분기가 아닌 4Q 에 반영될 것으로 예상한
다. 해양 부문에서 트리온 FPU 프로젝트 C/O에 따른 일부 환입 발생한 것으로 추정
되며, 해양 도크에서 컨테이너선 선체 블록 일부 건조로 고정비 감소효과 일부 상쇄 중
이다. 다만 올해 연내 혹은 내년 초 추가 해양 수주 없을 시 고정비 상승 우려 확대될 
전망이다. 동사 올해 상선 수주 목표(114.7억불)를 이미 초과하는 수주 실적 기록한 상
황이며, 고마진 선종인 LNGC 및 VLGC/VLAC 중심으로 수주했기 때문에 ‘28~’29
년 구간 경쟁사 대비 뚜렷한 선종 믹스 효과를 기대한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 82 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 97 만원에서 82
만원으로 하향한다. 중국의 공격적인 캐파 증설 지속과 더불어 중국 대형 조선사들의 
글로벌 선주가 발주한 LNGC 물량 및 원가 경쟁력 확대에 따른 상선 부문의 멀티플 
조정이 불가피하다는 판단이다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클
과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지
속되고 있는 중으로 파악된다. 올해 4 분기부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 부문의 본격적
인 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 전망이며, 해당 모멘텀에 따른 주가 상승
여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)를 유지한다.

## Document 278: Anthropic IPO最大赢家或是亚马逊：可转债账面价值达979亿美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic IPO最大赢家或是亚马逊：可转债账面价值达979亿美元.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

亚⻢逊凭借可转债结构，账⾯浮盈远超所有⻛投机构 ——62 亿美元本⾦撬动 979 亿美元估
值，最多可转换 18.4 亿股，持股上限 12% 。⾕歌同样坐享约 5.6% 股权及 1140 亿美元云计算
合同。反观 Menlo 、 Sequoia 等顶级⻛投，账⾯收益虽丰却⽆⼀突破 5% 披露⻔槛，折射出
科技巨头对 AI 独⻆兽的深度掌控逻辑。
摘要：
Anthropic 上市在即，众多⻛险投资机构将从中获益数⼗亿美元，但真正的最⼤赢家很可能另有
其⼈ —— 亚⻢逊。
据雅虎财经 10 ⽉ 27 ⽇报道，凭借独特的可转债结构安排，亚⻢逊对 Anthropic 的持仓账⾯价值已
⾼达 979 亿美元，远超任何⼀家⻛投机构，堪称这场 AI 独⻆兽上市盛宴中的头号受益者。
根据 Anthropic 提交的 S-1 招股⽂件，亚⻢逊持有本⾦余额 62 亿美元的可转债，这也是 Anthropic
⽬前的全部债务。然⽽，随着 Anthropic 估值在多轮融资中持续飙升，这批可转债所对应的股份
价值⼤幅膨胀。截⾄ 2026 年 6 ⽉ 30 ⽇，亚⻢逊在⾃⾝财务⽂件中将上述可转债持仓估值为 979 亿

美元。受此影响， Anthropic 在 2026 年上半年的损益表中， “ 可转债、衍⽣负债及 SAFE 公允价值
变动 ” ⼀项录得⾼达 724 亿美元的⾮现⾦费⽤。
与此同时，⾕歌同样将从 Anthropic 上市中获得丰厚回报。多家⻛投机构虽然绝对收益可观，但
在持股⽐例上均未达到 5% 的主要股东披露⻔槛，折射出 Anthropic 近万亿美元估值之下股权结构
的⾼度稀释。
⻛投机构收益丰厚，却⽆⼀跻⾝主要股东之列
Anthropic 的 S-1 ⽂件揭⽰了⼀个罕⻅现象：包括 Menlo Ventures 、 Sequoia 、 Sound Ventures
和 Spark Capital 在内的多家知名⻛投机构，均未达到 5% 的主要股东披露⻔槛。这在科技公司
IPO 中极为罕⻅ —— 通常情况下，领投⻛投机构会持有被投企业两位数百分⽐的股权。
原因在于 Anthropic 的体量。以 965 亿美元的私募估值计算， 1% 的股权价值接近 100 亿美元。据
彭博此前报道， Menlo Ventures 持仓约 140 亿美元，但这仅相当于 Anthropic 约 1.5% 的股权。换

⾔之，即便是账⾯收益最为丰厚的⻛投机构，在持股⽐例上也显得微不⾜道。
亚⻢逊：可转债结构锁定超额收益
亚⻢逊早在 2023 年便承诺向 Anthropic 投资最⾼ 40 亿美元，此后持续加码。与传统股权投资不
同，亚⻢逊将⼤部分投资以可转债形式结构化 —— 这类债务将在 IPO 或出售时转换为股权。
S-1 ⽂件显⽰，这批可转债最多可转换为 18.4 亿股 Anthropic 股票。不过，转换上限将亚⻢逊持股
⽐例限制在 Anthropic" 部分稀释 " 股本的 12% 以内，亚⻢逊可选择放弃该上限。超出上限⽆法转
换的部分将继续以债务形式存续，并在后续季度陆续转换，同样受上述限制约束。
可转债之外，亚⻢逊还在今年第⼆季度额外投⼊ 100 亿美元认购⽆投票权优先股，分别参与
Anthropic 最近两轮各 50 亿美元的融资。加上 2023 年⾄ 2025 年间投⼊可转债的 80 亿美元，亚⻢
逊对 Anthropic 的总投资额约达 180 亿美元。
云计算承诺叠加，亚⻢逊收益远不⽌于股权

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

图⽚
股权收益之外，亚⻢逊还将持续从 Anthropic 的业务扩张中获取可观的商业回报。Anthropic 已
承诺在未来⼗年内，在 AWS 上累计⽀出⾄少 1100 亿美元。 这意味着亚⻢逊不仅是 Anthropic 的
最⼤股东之⼀，更是其最重要的云计算服务供应商。
⾕歌同样坐拥双重收益。据《纽约时报》援引法庭⽂件报道，⾕歌在去年初持有 Anthropic 约
14% 的股权，此后继续加码，今年 4 ⽉再度投⼊ 100 亿美元。 PitchBook 分析师 Harrison Rolfes 向
TechCrunch 表⽰，经过多轮⼤规模融资的稀释，他估计⾕歌⽬前持股⽐例约为 5.6% 。与此同
时，⾕歌已获得 Anthropic ⾄ 2033 年累计⾄少 1140 亿美元的云计算承诺。

战略投资者主导，潜在利益冲突引发关注
Anthropic 的 S-1 ⽂件本⾝也对上述结构发出了⻛险提⽰。⽂件指出，部分云计算服务商同时在构
建竞争性 AI 模型，其利益诉求未必始终与 Anthropic ⼀致。
这⼀格局清晰呈现出 Anthropic 上市的深层逻辑：真正主导这场 IPO 收益分配的，并⾮传统意义
上的⻛险投资机构，⽽是亚⻢逊、⾕歌这样的科技巨头 —— 它们既是 Anthropic 的早期投资者，
⼜是其最⼤的基础设施供应商，在财务回报与商业绑定上实现了双重锁定。

## Document 291: Bank of Canada likely to hike again after today’s move

Source PDF: all report pdf/Bank of Canada likely to hike again after today’s move.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The BoC raised the policy rate by 25bp to 2.5% today, matching
market expectations. Policymakers acknowledge rising
inﬂationary risks, and while not recommitting to a rate path,
the indications are that this won’t be a one-and-done hike. We
expect the next move in 1Q27, with risks of an earlier one in
December. We remain cautious on CAD's upside risks

James Knightley and Francesco Pesole
The BoC raised rates to 2.5% today
BoC to proceed carefully after hike
The Bank of Canada has raised its target for the overnight rate by
25bp to 2.5%. Ahead of the meeting, it was about 80% priced by
markets, with economists split down the middle on whether we
would get a hike or not – the Canadian outlook is improving, but
inﬂation is in line with target and the recent jobs numbers have
been disappointing with the unemployment rate rising to 6.5%.
The statement justiﬁes the action on the basis that the recent
growth ﬁgures have been stronger than anticipated thanks in part

### Page 2

to "solid" consumer spending, while business capex in the oil and
gas industry has rebounded on higher energy prices. They also
note that sectors that had been heavily impacted by US tariﬀs have
strengthened.
In an environment of rising cost pressures, which so far have been
largely absorbed by businesses, there is a sense that cost pass-
through could be more of a theme for 2027. As such, inﬂation could
be more persistent than previously thought likely. Given this

situation, the BoC concluded that "there could be the need to
increase the policy rate further..." but they will "proceed carefully".
Market expectations still too hawkish
Markets are currently seeing the next meeting in December as a
50:50 call on whether we get another hike. Right now our forecast is
for the BoC to hike rates to 2.75% in the ﬁrst quarter of 2027 (our
preference is January over March) and hold them steady, but the
risk is that they go a little more aggressively, and we see a

December hike followed by a 1Q27 move.
Anyway, we still judge market expectations for 90bp of tightening
over the next 12 months as overly hawkish. The BoC has a central
estimate for the neutral rate around 2.75%, and we agree with their
estimate that second-round eﬀects will be present but overall
contained, especially in terms of wage growth spillover.
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
CAD outlook is very oil-dependent

In the near term, elevated oil prices risk prompting an upward
surprise in inﬂation and leading markets to price in a December

hike. The BoC can accommodate this. This adds to the weight that
oil prices have on CAD’s near-term outlook. We remain on the more
bearish side of the oil story, and think a decline in energy prices
could lead to broad dovish repricing and release pressure on the
BoC to hike in December.
We remain unconvinced that CAD can draw much more beneﬁt
from the domestic story, and only see USD/CAD trading steadily
below 1.40 should USD-related risks build up. Our target for year-

end for the pair remains unchanged, with some upside risks to
1.420.

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -29.18 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Dịch vụ phân phối là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -3,013 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã MCH, VIC và FPT; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 
887 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -2,021 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -4,384 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng -
4,275 hợp đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -7,815 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ tư liên tiếp, khi VN-Index lùi về đóng cửa 1.706 
điểm - giảm khoảng 1,7% so với tuần trước và đang dao động quanh vùng hỗ trợ tâm lý 
1.700 điểm, cũng là vùng đáy cũ của tháng 8. Thanh khoản thị trường tiếp tục suy yếu, 
phản ánh tâm lý thận trọng cao độ trong bối cảnh thị trường thiếu vắng thông tin hỗ trợ 
trong nước, trong khi các yếu tố bên ngoài như xung đột tại Iran, và chi phí vốn toàn cầu 
chưa hạ nhiệt tiếp tục đè nặng tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Khối ngoại duy trì chuỗi bán ròng kéo 
dài nhiều phiên liên tiếp, nối dài đà rút vốn đã lên tới xấp xỉ 95 nghìn tỷ đồng từ đầu năm. 
Trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo kết quả kinh doanh quý 3 được kỳ vọng là chất xúc 
tác giúp thị trường giao dịch cân bằng trở lại.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành mẫu nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên đồ thị tuần, đánh dấu tuần 
giảm thứ 4 liên tiếp, cho thấy áp lực phân phối vẫn đang có phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, 
tuy chỉ số đã có nỗ lực phục hồi trở lại trong 3 phiên đan xen, đặc biệt là phản ứng trong 
phiên cuối tuần khi chạm trendline trung hạn, nhưng việc chỉ có thể hình thành các mẫu 
nến "spinning" hàm ý đà tích lũy chưa thực sự thuyết phục. Trong một vài phiên kế tiếp, 
VNINDEX có thể xuất hiện nhịp hồi kỹ thuật từ vùng hỗ trợ, tương ứng với cạnh dưới tam 
giác, nhưng sau khi chớm phá vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1710 và hiện chỉ còn một số ít các mã giữ 
được xu hướng tăng tính từ đáy tháng 7, xu hướng ngắn hạn đang trở nên khá trung tính.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

## Document 320: Czech National Bank preview_ Another hike, more on the horizon

Source PDF: all report pdf/Czech National Bank preview_ Another hike, more on the horizon.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

We expect the CNB to hike rates by 25bp to 4.00% on 5
November, with hawkish forecast revisions leaving further
tightening on the table. Higher energy and food prices and
wage costs underpin our above-consensus inﬂation outlook.
The koruna should outperform CEE peers, while rates' pricing
should limit any hawkish surprises
Frantisek Taborsky
The Czech National Bank in Prague
CNB set to hike rates again
We expect the Czech National Bank (CNB) to hike rates by 25bp to

4.00% next Thursday, the second such move since the onset of the
US-Iran conﬂict. The inﬂation outlook has deteriorated in recent
months, even though headline inﬂation remains comfortably
within the tolerance band (2.5% in September). However, both
headline and core inﬂation have exceeded the CNB’s forecasts, and
the outlook points to an upward trajectory. The CNB will have a new
forecast at its disposal, which we believe will support the Bank

### Page 2

Board’s decision to hike rates again and signal further increases to
come.
The CNB has scaled back its forward guidance. Prior to the start of
the blackout period, we observed only two interviews with board
members. Deputy Governor Jan Frait stated that “arguments for a
rate hike make sense,” though he noted that no decision has been
made yet and that there are also reasons to keep rates unchanged.
Board member Jakub Seidler indicated that he leans toward a rate

hike at the November meeting. While Frait is occasionally an outlier
within the board, Seidler has consistently voted in line with the
board’s consensus, giving us conﬁdence that a rate hike will be
delivered next week.
4.00% CNB key rate
25bp rate hike expected
Hawkish forecast revisions point to further
rate hikes
The CNB’s new forecast is expected to feature a series of hawkish
upgrades. Headline and core inﬂation have run slightly above the
forecast in recent months, which marginally raises the starting

point. External assumptions are also likely to undergo a signiﬁcant
hawkish revision. In the new forecast, eurozone CPI and PPI are
expected to be signiﬁcantly higher, while the EURIBOR projection—
based on forward rates—has shifted upward by roughly 80bp on
average across the entire horizon of the CNB’s forecast compared to
the August outlook. Based on current forward prices, the CNB’s
forecast for oil prices will have to be raised by 21% for next year

and by 11% for 2028 relative to the August forecast. The outlook
for gas prices is even more pronounced, with an upward revision of
80% for next year and 60% for 2028. Furthermore, the CNB must
incorporate the government’s draft budget for next year, which
anticipates a 6.8% average increase in public sector wages and an
11.2% hike in the minimum wage.

### Page 3

Collectively, these factors should lead to a notable revision of the
inﬂation outlook for next year; in the August version, the CNB had
projected an average of 2.8% for the ﬁrst quarter of 2027, with a
subsequent slowdown. At the same time, faster wage growth and
robust consumption are expected to push core inﬂation higher than
previously anticipated. This should also be reﬂected in a higher
interest rate path—noting that the current outlook does not factor

in any further rate hikes. The only 'dovish' signal in the new forecast
is likely the lower GDP growth, which, for 2Q26, came in 0.4
percentage points below the CNB’s previous expectations.
Nevertheless, the overall tone of the forecast is expected to be
'hawkish,' pointing toward further rate hikes in the future.
Higher energy prices work their way
through the price chain
Source: Macrobond, ING
We remain more hawkish on inﬂation than
the CNB and the rest of the market

All the aforementioned adjustments to the CNB’s forecast align
with our own projections. We expect GDP growth of 1.8% this year
and 2.3% next year, well below the CNB’s ﬁgures of 2.2% and 2.7%,
respectively. At the same time, we anticipate inﬂation to average
3.9% in the ﬁrst quarter of 2027 and 3.4% for the entirety of next
year, well above the CNB and rest of the market estimates. This is
driven primarily by a turnaround in food prices, which had

### Page 4

previously helped keep headline inﬂation in check, as well as higher
energy costs, which will feed into the CPI via regulated prices,
particularly in January during the annual price adjustments.
Overall, we expect a rate hike at the November meeting, and we
remain open to incorporating further hikes into our forecast,
depending on the CNB’s communication next week. In any case, we
believe that, following the November meeting, the December and,
most notably, the February meetings remain 'live' for further

tightening, contingent upon incoming inﬂation data, especially the
January print.
Our market views
Although we have been bearish on CEE currencies since the
beginning of September, the koruna stands out as an outlier.
It should remain supported by the central bank’s hawkish
stance and we remain bullish. The CNB is the only central
bank in the CEE region that has already responded to the
onset of the US-Iran conﬂict with rate hikes, and according to

our forecast, it will continue to do so in the coming months.
The market is pricing in more than 100bp of rate hikes, and
we do not expect this to change, given the continued rise in
energy prices on global markets. This should continue to
provide a suﬃcient buﬀer for the CZK, shielding it from the
global pressures currently weighing on its CEE peers.
Furthermore, the Czech Republic stands out within the region
in terms of ﬁscal indicators, which should help insulate it

from spillovers originating in the eurozone bond market. On
the other hand, following the CZK rally in October, we believe
that EUR/CZK is currently fairly priced in the 24.300–24.350
range, barring any signiﬁcant surprise from the CNB relative
to our already hawkish expectations. We maintain our view
that the CZK should outperform its CEE peers, unless there is
a substantial easing of global risk premiums supporting the
region.

A rate hike by the CNB in   November is almost fully priced in,
and the market is anticipating another hike at the February

meeting, with a terminal rate of 5.00% expected over a
horizon of roughly a year and a half. Even relative to our own
hawkish forecast, this is signiﬁcantly higher than expected.
This sets the bar for a hawkish surprise very high; in fact, CNB
meetings this year have typically resulted in a dovish
adjustment to market pricing. Global energy prices remain
the primary driver for local rates pricing, with the CNB
exerting only limited inﬂuence; consequently, we do not

anticipate a signiﬁcant impact on that front. For the time
being, the yield curve is more likely to steepen from current
levels before shifting toward a ﬂattening phase early next
year, coinciding with an expected jump in inﬂation.
Furthermore, we believe the CZK market should price in the
most aggressive rate hikes within the region, and that the
spread against the PLN is set to widen further, reﬂecting a
distinct reaction function.

## Document 394: NZ labour market preview, Q3 2026 (2026-10-29)

Source PDF: all report pdf/NZ labour market preview, Q3 2026 (2026-10-29).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ

### Page 1

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Turning point?
• We expect the unemployment rate to edge
down to 5.5% for the September quarter.
• Job numbers have been gaining momentum
in recent months, and now look to be enough
to outstrip population growth. However, the
participation rate will be the deciding factor.
• Despite cost-of-living pressures on workers,
the existing slack in the labour market is likely
to keep wage growth subdued.
• Our forecasts are a little more positive than

what the RBNZ assumed in their September
policy statement. Assuming they deliver on
an OCR hike this afternoon, the labour market
surveys could have an important bearing on
whether they follow up with another hike in
December.

The New Zealand economy has returned to growth over
the last year, despite a number of headwinds. That’s been
accompanied by some modest growth in jobs, though
to date it hasn’t been enough to prevent a further rise

in the unemployment rate. However, we think that next
Wednesday’s labour market reports could show the jobs
market reaching a turning point.
We expect the unemployment rate to ease from 5.6%
to 5.5% in the September quarter, which would mark
the first fall in almost five years. That’s not a firmly held
view, given the usual margin of uncertainty around this
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
Preview of Q3 labour market surveys:
4 November, 10:45am.
28 Oct 2026 | Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
measure. But we’re now seeing consistent evidence
that jobs growth is outstripping the modest pace of
population growth. We also note that the previous result
was 5.56% in unrounded terms, so it wouldn’t take much
to tip the rounded rate down to 5.5% this time.
On the whole, our forecasts are a little stronger than
what the RBNZ assumed in its September Monetary
Policy Statement. While our unemployment rate forecast

is identical, we’re more positive on both employment
and labour force participation. Meanwhile, our pick of a
0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index is slightly ahead of the
RBNZ’s estimate of 0.4%.
Assuming that the RBNZ delivers the OCR hike that we
expect this afternoon, we think they will be noncommittal
about the chances of another hike at the 9 December
Monetary Policy Statement. In that respect, the labour
market surveys could be important in setting market

expectations for the December review, as there aren’t
that many major data releases between now and then.
Forecast details.
We expect the Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS)
to show a 0.5% rise in employment for the September
quarter. That’s broadly in line with the Monthly
Employment Indicator (MEI), with the September release
this morning showing a third straight month of growth
in filled jobs. We note that employment growth in the
HLFS has actually outstripped the MEI for the last nine

quarters, but it’s not clear why this has been the case and
we’re wary of extrapolating it too far into the next quarter.

Employment growth of around 0.5% would be more than
enough to absorb the modest 0.3% rise in the working-
age population, and on its own would suggest a fall in

the unemployment rate. But it also comes down to the
rate of labour force participation, which we don’t really
have much guidance on. The participation rate has been
choppy over recent quarters, and in the June quarter
we saw an unusually large 0.3pt rise to 70.7%; we’re
assuming some payback this time with a more modest
0.1pt rise to 70.8%.
We’ll also be keeping an eye on the broader indicators of
slack in the labour market, although we don’t formally use

these in our forecasts. The June quarter saw a sharp rise
in the underutilisation rate from 12.9% to 13.8%. However,
we think there’s some residual seasonality in this series,
relating to one component in particular: people who are
available for work but not actively looking. In that case,
we could see a substantial pullback in the underutilisation
measure this time.
Previously we’ve characterised the labour market as a
“low-fire, low-hire” environment, with much of the rise in

unemployment reflecting the difficulty of breaking into
the workforce, rather than job losses. There are some
signs of a thaw emerging, with job vacancies picking up
from their lows over the last year and business surveys
pointing to a lift in hiring intentions. But we still expect
that it will be a long road to recovery – our current
forecasts see the unemployment rate at 5% by the end of
next year.
While cost-of-living pressures are again rearing their

head as a major concern for workers, the existing degree
of slack in the labour market will continue to make it
difficult for them to push for bigger pay increases. We
expect a 0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index (LCI) for
the September quarter, which would nudge the annual
growth rate up slightly to 2.1%. That said, we don’t have a
lot to go on for this forecast. The MEI includes a measure
of median weekly earnings, which has shown no sign of
acceleration so far – but this is only a very loose indicator

of wage pressures, as it will also reflect any changes in
the composition of jobs and pay rates.

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank of Thailand (BOT) (October 28)

We expect the BOT to maintain its policy 
rate at 1.00% at its 28 October meeting, 
extending its pause for a fourth 
consecutive decision as it balances 
inflationary pressures against uneven 
and subdued economic growth. Our view 
is aligned with fixed market income 
pricing. Headline inflation rose anew to 
2.8% yoy in September 2026, driven by 
higher energy, food, and core price 
increases, but remained within the upper 
end of the central bank’s 1 -3% target 
range. As the BOT is still likely to look 
through what it views as temporary, 
supply-driven inflationary pressu res, it 
will likely refrain from tightening policy, 
and keep interest rates accommodative 
to support the soft and uneven growth 
cycle.

European Central Bank (October 29)

We expect the ECB to extend its pause, 
keeping the deposit facility rate at 2.5%. 
Market participants will instead focus on 
the central bank’s assessment of rising 
French and Italian bond yields and 
widening spreads versus Bunds. While 
we do not expect dir ect references to 
individual countries, the ECB is likely to 
signal that recent developments in 
France reflect the country’s weaker 
fiscal and debt profile rather than a 
broader systemic concern. Policymakers 
will seek to differentiate between a 
localised bond sell -off and a Eurozone -
wide market disruption, justifying their 
decision to monitor developments rather 
than implement specific measures at this 
juncture. Meanwhile, inflation continues 
to rise, with little relief expected in 
upcoming readings given elevated 
global fuel prices. 
Bank of Japan (October 30)

The Bank of Japan is expected to keep 
the overnight call rate unchanged at 1.25% 
at this meeting, pausing after the 25bp 
hike in September. The BOJ is likely to 
assess the impact of the previous rate 
hike before taking further action. The 
recent stabilisat ion of the yen also 
reduces the urgency of another hike at 
this meeting.

The tightening bias should remain intact. 
Growth and inflation remain broadly on 
track, leaving room for the BOJ to revise 
up its FY2026 –FY2027 GDP and CPI 
forecasts. Wage growth has also 
remained strong, with Rengo reportedly 
targeting wage increases of 6% in 2027, 
reinforcing the BOJ’s confidence that 
underlying inflation can be sustained 
around its 2% target. We continue to 
expect the policy rate to rise to 1.50% in 
December 2026 or January 2027, 
followed by a further increase to 1.75% 
by 2Q 2027.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Hong Kong SAR

Export growth is expected to accelerate 
from 53.0% yoy in August to 55.0% in 
September, in line with the pickup in 
China’s export growth from 25.0% to 
36.3%. As a key re-export hub for China,

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Hong Kong should benefit from robust 
AI-related electronics trade, with 
electrical machinery, equipment and 
parts exports to Hong Kong accounting 
for 20.6% of China’s total exports. 
Import growth is also expected to remain 
firm, supported by resilient expo rt 
orders and gradually improving 
investment sentiment, sustaining 
demand for intermediate and capital 
goods.

Singapore

Singapore’s industrial production (IP) 
likely remained in expansion for a 13th 
consecutive month in September 2026, 
although we expect growth to moderate 
to 7.6% yoy from August’s strong 15.4% 
yoy pace. IP growth continued to be 
underpinned by the electronics and 
precision engineering clusters, which 
has been key drivers of Singapore’s 
manufacturing strength, reflecting 
sustained demand for artificial 
intelligence-related hardware. However, 
performance across clusters remained 
uneven, with chemicals and biomedical 
manufacturing likely contracting, and 
tempering overall gains.

South Korea

The preliminary estimate for 3Q26 GDP 
is likely to show growth remaining solid 
at 3.2% yoy , slightly below 3.7% in 2Q 
and 3.8% in 1Q. Nominal GDP growth is 
likely to remain in double digits, 
significantly outpacing real GDP growth, 
thanks to improved terms of trade amid 
rising memory export prices. By 
expenditure, exports picked up in 3Q in 
both nominal and real terms, supported 
by stronger semiconductor shipments. 
Investment also appeared robust, as 
reflected in the equipment investment 
estimation index and machinery order 
index. Private consumption, however, 
appeared to weaken amid the sharp 
KOSPI correction since July following  
the introduction of leveraged ETF 
regulations. Our full -year GDP growth 
forecasts of 3.5% for 2026 and 2.4% for 
2027 remain on track.

Taiwan

The preliminary estimate for 3Q GDP is 
likely to show growth remaining strong 
at 12 –13% yoy , close to 12.9% in 2Q, 
albeit below the peak of 15.4% in 1Q. 
Exports maintained strong double -digit 
growth in 3Q (44.6% yoy  in nominal 
terms and 21.1% in real terms), supported 
by robust shipments of semiconductors, 
servers and other AI -related products. 
Machinery investment also sustained 
strong double-digit growth, underpinned 
by higher capacity utilisation in the ICT 
sector and a resulting increase in capital 
goods imports. Private consumption 
appeared to have strengthened further, 
supported by stock market gains, 
improving wage growth, and the 
associated wealth and income effects. 
Construction investment remained the 
weak s pot, weighed down by elevated 
inventories in the new-home market and 
the ongoing need for inventory digestion. 
We maintain our 2026 and 2027 GDP 
growth forecasts at 11.6% and 5.6%, 
respectively, with risks tilted to the 
upside.

## Document 444: Top of Mind - Higher Bond Yields_ How Concerning_

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Global bond yields have risen to multi-decade highs, yet growth and markets have
remained remarkably resilient. But with some pressures beginning to emerge (e.g. in
European sovereign bond markets), can this resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities
be revealed? We ask the IMF’s Tobias Adrian, Moody’s Analytics’ Mark Zandi, and GS
economists. Adrian is relatively sanguine about the economy’s resilience, though
notes downside risks, while GS economists expect only a modest growth drag from

higher rates, but a more consequential one if yields remain near current or higher
levels. Zandi is generally more concerned, describing the US economy as vulnerable
rather than resilient, and sees one-in-three odds of the economy going off the r ails.
Meanwhile, GS strategists and analysts are not especially concerned about banks or
markets more broadly, but warn that further increases in yields or rate volatility could
test risk assets. Key to watch: whether the increasingly interconnected and leveraged

AI investment boom can continue to withstand higher rates.
Tobias Adrian, Special Advisor and former Director of Monetary and Capital Markets, IMF
“The global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, pr ovided they do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same
time, I do worry about downside risks.”
Mark Zandi, Chief Economist, Moody’s Analytics

### Page 3

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Recent acceleration in the rise in global bond yields to multi-
decade highs has begun to raise questions about the 
implications for growth and markets. Both have remained 
remarkably resilient so far, with US growth in particular 
holding relatively firm and the S&P 500 and other equity 
markets near new all-time highs. But with pressure 
beginning to emerge—most notably in European sovereign 
bond markets—and yields looking unlikely to retreat soon, 
can that resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities be revealed? 
GS Head of US Rates Strategy William Marshall first lays out 
how we arrived at higher rates, underscoring the striking 
breadth of the rise, which he attributes mainly to 
fundamentals—strong growth, inflation risks, hawkish policy 
shifts, the ongoing investment cycle, and long-run fiscal 
risks—but notes that technical factors likely amplified the 
recent move. And while he ultimately expects yields to fall 
(with YE 10-year US Treasury yields of 4.75%/4.4% in 
2026/2027, respectively) as markets become more confident 
in easing inflation, he thinks elevated long-term yields should 
have some staying power, and sees continued upside risk 
from energy price and fiscal pressures.  
So, just how much of a threat do higher rates pose to the 
macro outlook? For answers, we first turn to Tobias Adrian, 
Special Advisor to the Managing Director and former 
Financial Counsellor and Director of the Monetary and Capital 
Markets Department at the IMF, Mark Zandi, Chief 
Economist of Moody’s Analytics, and GS US economists. 
Adrian sees a global economy that has so far responded to 
the yield shock much as it has to the many other major 
shocks of the past seven years: resiliently. He notes that the 
rise in yields has not significantly tightened financial 
conditions or, more importantly, disrupted market functioning 
or forced damaging deleveraging. He believes that resilience 
could persist as long as the move in yields doesn’t accelerate 
or become more disorderly. But he is concerned about 
vulnerabilities in the AI and technology complex, sovereign 
bond markets, and EMs—not to mention cyber risks—all of 
which leaves him “vigilant but cautiously optimistic.” 
GS US economists also characterize the US economy as 
resilient. Their expectation that the Fed will deliver just one 
more rate hike this year before cutting next year—consistent 
with Marshall’s expectation of lower Treasury yields—leads 
Pierfrancesco Mei to expect only a modest 0.2pp growth 
drag in 2027 from higher rates. But he finds that the drag 
could rise to a more consequential 0.5pp if yields remain near 
current levels (see pg. 9 for GS global economists Joseph 
Briggs’ and Johan Allen’s rules of thumb for the impact of 
higher yields on rate-sensitive areas across major DMs.) 
David Mericle and Mei also worry about the implications for 
the already-concerning US fiscal trajectory, which they 
believe is unlikely to trigger a classic debt crisis but is too risky 
to ignore.     
Zandi, for his part, is generally more concerned. While his 
baseline outlook is relatively sanguine, he describes the US 
economy as vulnerable rather than resilient and views the 
“distribution of possible outcomes as very flat and heavily 
skewed toward darker tail scenarios.” He sees the clearest 
softness in the labor market, with weak hiring and falling real 
wage growth for many workers weighing on consumer 
spending. And he takes little comfort from US growth 
continuing to run around 2%—roughly its potential rate—
noting that AI accounts for roughly one-third of that growth, 
leaving the non-AI economy growing at only around 1-1.5%, 
which he calls “a pretty punk economy.” Zandi also worries 
that the US fiscal situation is “as bad as I have ever seen it” 
and that higher rates are already inflicting damage on real 
estate markets and squeezing existing debtors. As these 
stresses mount, he sees elevated risk of something breaking 
in the financial system. All told, he puts the odds of the 
current vulnerabilities pushing the economy completely off 
the rails at around one-in-three.  
With banks historically at the center of the rate shocks and 
economic crises that Zandi is worried about, we then explore 
how much risk banks pose today. Adrian and GS Head of the 
Financials Equity Group Richard Ramsden both see little 
cause for alarm, explaining that higher rates should boost 
banks’ net interest income and revenues given their asset-
sensitive balance sheets. Ramsden also doesn’t expect a 
significant deterioration in credit quality absent a broader 
economic downturn and says that most banks should have 
excess capital even after accounting for rate-driven portfolio 
losses.   
But even if banks can weather higher rates, what about 
markets more broadly? GS strategists and analysts are not 
especially concerned, with Chief US Equity Strategist Ben 
Snider arguing that current rate levels shouldn’t prevent 
continued strong US earnings growth and the S&P 500 from 
climbing higher, Chief Credit Strategist Amanda Lynam 
viewing the headwind to corporate credit from higher rates 
as manageable amid the solid growth backdrop, and US 
Financials Analyst Alexander Blostein expecting the recent 
slowdown in private market activity to rebound once rates 
stabilize. GS multi-asset strategists Christian Mueller-
Glissmann and Andrea Ferrario add that higher yields have 
strengthened the strategic case for bonds in a multi-asset 
portfolio, though strong earnings continue to favor equities 
for now. But they all warn that if rate volatility remains 
elevated, and especially if yields continue to rise from here, 
risk assets could face mounting pressures.  
All that said, with the AI boom powering the economy and 
markets, everything may come down to whether higher rates 
will derail it. Snider views this as unlikely given the strong 
balance sheets of the firms investing in AI and the expected 
profitability of these investments, with Lynam also seeing a 
high bar to derail AI-related debt issuance. Adrian also 
maintains a positive baseline for the AI economy but is 
concerned about the circularity of investment, increased 
leverage, and the sustainability of revenue growth in the AI 
complex. And Zandi again seems the most worried, warning 
that “AI could become the part of the economy that breaks” 
if rates remain at current levels for much longer.  
Allison Nathan, Editor  
Email: allison.nathan@gs.com
